## 0. Read-first protocol (for any AI or human picking this project up)

1. Read **this file end to end**. It is capped at 500 lines; nothing here is optional.
2. Read the PRD sections named in your prompt's `AUTHORITATIVE INPUTS`. Do not read the whole PRD unless asked.
3. Read `AGENTS.md` for standing engineering rules.
4. Check §5 (build state) for what exists, §9 (deviations) for where reality already differs from the PRD, and §10 (open questions + known PRD conflicts) before you form any assumption.
5. **Precedence when sources conflict:**
   `AGENTS.md` (process) → §2 Invariants → §8 Decision Log (highest ADR number wins) → PRD → §5 Build State → anything else.
   A ledger ADR beats the PRD **only if** it names the PRD section it overrides in its `Overrides` column. Otherwise the PRD wins and the ledger is wrong — fix the ledger.
6. Never silently resolve a conflict. PRD-internal contradictions go in §10 with a ruling; code-vs-PRD divergence goes in §9. Surface it in your response either way.

**Operational prompts (`OP-n`) — defined here because §5, §11 and `AGENTS.md` §2 referenced them before anything said what they were.** They are not build prompts: none of them appears in the §5 roadmap, and any of them may run at any time.

| ID | Purpose | Reads | Writes | Trigger |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **OP-1** | **Re-plan.** Re-sequence remaining prompts, re-price the scope-cut order (§12), re-check the hard floor against elapsed time | `CONTEXT.md`, PRD §40 | §5, §7, §12 + an ADR if the sequence changes | Schedule slip, or a prompt discovering that a later one is now impossible |
| **OP-2** | **Independent audit** of a `BUILT` module against its prompt's `DELIVERABLES`, the invariants it claimed to hold, and its acceptance gate. **Read-only: it changes no code.** It produces findings, and it must run each gate itself rather than trusting the build session's report | The module, its prompt, `CONTEXT.md`, the cited PRD sections | §5 `Status` → `VERIFIED` (or findings that trigger OP-3), §13 `Audit` column | A module reaches `BUILT`. Required before `VERIFIED`; **not** required before the next build prompt starts |
| **OP-3** | **Repair.** Fixes a defect found by an §11 tripwire or an OP-2 finding. Scoped to the named defect; it is not a licence to refactor | The finding, the offending module | The module + a regression test that fails before the fix (`AGENTS.md` §5) + §9 if it declares a deviation | An §11 tripwire fires, or OP-2 reports a finding |

**Best run by a different model than built the module** — an auditor that shares the builder's blind spots inherits them. `VERIFIED` means an OP-2 audit passed; a module the building session merely reports as working is `BUILT`, never `VERIFIED`.

---

## 1. Project identity — the one paragraph that must never drift

AgentDX is a **pre-deployment reliability and coordination-debugging system for multi-agent AI applications**. It takes an existing agent graph (LangGraph, or any Python multi-agent system wrapped with the decorator API), executes it under a **deterministic cooperative scheduler driven by a virtual clock**, serves every LLM call from a **record/replay cache**, optionally injects **controlled faults**, records every observable action into an **append-only event log**, and then runs **pure analytical passes over that log** to answer one question: *is this multi-agent system actually better than one agent doing the same work — and if not, exactly where did the time, tokens and correctness go?*

The thesis obliges eleven things to be **measurable, not merely visible** (PRD §1.2, table M1–M11). Every module traces to a row in that table. Work that serves no row is negotiable.

**The product is not an observability dashboard.** Observability watches production and shows what happened. AgentDX runs the system under controlled conditions before deployment and tells you *why*, reproducibly, offline, for free.

**If a proposed change makes the system less deterministic, less evidence-backed, or more like a generic tracing UI, it is the wrong change.**

---

## 2. Invariants — violating any of these is a project-level failure

Admission test: violating it invalidates the product thesis (§1) or a named acceptance gate (§6). Good practices are not invariants; they live in `AGENTS.md`.

| # | Invariant | Mechanically enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| **I1** | **Determinism.** Same seed + same cache + same scenario → byte-identical *canonical projection* (PRD §10.7) of the event log. 100/100 replays, ≥10 in fresh processes. | Gate G3, `tests/determinism/` |
| **I2** | **The event log is append-only and immutable.** Nothing edits or deletes an event after write. Analysis never mutates. | SQLite triggers in `store/sqlite.py` + kill-test suite |
| **I3** | **Analysis is pure.** `agentdx.analysis.*` must not import `agentdx.runtime.*` or `agentdx.sdk.*` — **no module is exempt, `analysis.baseline` included.** Baseline is the one analyser that must *execute* a run, and it does so via a `BaselineExecutor` protocol injected by the caller: the protocol type is declared in `analysis`, the implementation is constructed in `cli`. Injection is the mechanism that **avoids** the import; it is not a licence to import (PRD §24.3, "rather than importing the runtime directly"). | import-linter in CI, with **no allowlist entry** for `analysis.baseline` |
| **I4** | **Zero false positives on the healthy fixture.** `research_fanout` yields an empty race-findings set across all 100 determinism replays **and** the k=2 exploration frontier. A tool that reports races in correct code is worse than no tool. **This invariant's own `research_fanout` is the real fixture under `fixtures/`; P13's FR-6 exploration engine (§5 row 13) has only demonstrated the equivalent property on a hand-built synthetic harness shaped like it, never the literal fixture — do not read §7's P13 paragraph or §6's G2 note as evidence toward this row.** | Gate G2, `tests/false_positives/` — including its own k=2 harness, which is P0 and independent of FR-6 (ADR-002) |
| **I5** | **Race precision = 1.0** on the labelled benchmark set. Recall may be < 1.0 and is reported honestly. Precision is never traded for recall. | PRD §34.3 benchmark, `bench/results/` |
| **I6** | **Every verdict, finding and scorecard line carries evidence** = concrete event `seq` references. An empty evidence array fails schema validation and cannot be rendered. | Verdict schema (PRD §18.4) |
| **I7** | **Offline by default.** The three-fixture demo runs with the network disabled and no API keys in the environment. A `replay`-mode cache miss is a hard error (`E-CACHE-001`, exit 3) — never a silent live call, and no "fall back to live" flag may exist. | Gate G9, NFR-4 |
| **I8** | **Privacy by default.** Prompt/response bodies are never written to the event log unless explicitly opted in. | NFR-6, automated plaintext scan of the DB |
| **I9** | **No statistic ships without a reproducible measurement** in `bench/results/` (Rule E1). | CI check: every published number carries a `[bench:<file>]` marker resolving to a committed result — see `AGENTS.md` §6 |
| **I10** | **The bounded-exploration coverage statement appears verbatim** — *"Bounded search: absence of findings is not proof of absence."* — in CLI output, API responses and the UI. Removing it is a release blocker. | Required `coverage_statement` API field + string test (PRD §15.6) |
| **I11** | **Virtual time and wall time are never conflated.** Any unqualified duration in code, output or docs is virtual time. `wall_ts_ms` and the other PRD §10.7 volatile fields are excluded from the canonical projection. | Canonical projection test; naming lint (`*_wall_ms` suffix required) |
| **I12** | **Chaos is fixture-only by default.** A user graph requires `chaos_opt_in: true` *in the scenario file* plus a non-empty blast radius; every fault carries a declared blast radius, steady-state hypothesis and abort guards. | Scenario validation (`E-SCEN-004`) + runtime re-check (`E-CHAOS-001`), PRD §13.3–13.6 |
| **I13** | **No model inference anywhere in the analysis, verdict or scoring path.** Every finding is produced by a deterministic algorithm. LLM-as-judge and embedding-based semantic dedup are out of scope (PRD §4.4, §43.3.2, §43.3.3); a model in this path breaks I1 and NFR-14 at once. | import-linter: `analysis.*` may not import any provider/SDK client; NFR-14 (same log analysed 100× → identical findings) |

*Candidates tested and rejected as invariants (they are gate criteria or standing rules, not thesis-level):* comparability grading of the baseline (PRD §17.5 — enforced by G6), the event-schema freeze (§3 + tripwire 6), single-OS-thread execution (a consequence of I1).

## 3. Locked decisions — do not renegotiate without an ADR

| Area | Locked value |
|---|---|
| Name | **AgentDX** (43.1.1) |
| Language / runtime | Python 3.12, asyncio, single OS thread for the scheduler; user-spawned threads are rejected in strict mode (PRD §10.6) |
| Agent framework | LangGraph primary + generic Python decorator API. **No CrewAI adapter in v1** (43.1.3) |
| Model for agent brains | Groq, Llama 3.1 8B, free tier, via an **OpenAI-compatible shim** |
| Event store | SQLite (WAL), append-only; DuckDB over exported Parquet for analytics (threshold ~20 000 events) |
| LLM cache | SQLite. Four modes: `record` / `replay` / `perturb` / `passthrough`. **`replay` is the default**; `passthrough` exists only for the NFR-1 overhead benchmark (PRD §11.2) |
| MVP fault set | `latency`, `agent_crash`, `message_drop`, `tool_failure` **only**. The other six fault types (reorder, duplicate, agent_slow, rate_limit, byzantine, state_corrupt) are P1 (PRD §4.1) |
| API | FastAPI + `websockets`, bound to `127.0.0.1:8420`, no auth; `--host` is required to bind elsewhere and prints a no-auth warning. OpenAPI 3.1 at `/openapi.json`; the frontend client is generated from it |
| CLI | Typer; exit codes 0–7 are **authoritative and stable** (PRD §37.2) — changing one is a breaking change |
| Frontend | React 18 + Vite + TypeScript + React Flow + Zustand + Tailwind + visx |
| Packaging | `uv` (+ hatch), Docker Compose, `just` task runner |
| Determinism guarantee | **Canonical projection equality**, not literal byte equality (43.1.6) |
| Event schema | Frozen at end of week 1. `causal_parents`, `span_id`, `sched_step`, `schema_version`, hash chain all adopted (43.1.5) |
| Scenario YAML | P0, week 2 (FR-11a). `--ci` mode is P1 (FR-11b) (43.1.2, PRD §4.5) |
| Byzantine faults | Measured via curated wrong-output fixtures + the system's response, **no judge model** (43.1.4) |
| Redundancy detection | Exact hash of `(tool_name ‖ canonical_json(args))` only in v1. No embedding similarity (43.3.2) |
| Exploration reduction | Independence-based reduction in v1; sleep sets / full DPOR only if redundancy > 40 % (43.2.4) |
| Thresholds & weights | `src/agentdx/analysis/verdict_rules.toml` — versioned, printable via `agentdx analyze --explain`. No threshold inline in code |
| Platforms | macOS (Apple silicon + Intel) and Linux x86_64/arm64, Python 3.12+. **Windows unsupported in v1** |
| Design tokens | `--navy-900 #0A2947`, `--cream #F3E4C9`, `--sage #D3D4C0`, `--clay #8B5E3C` + derived status tokens (PRD §29.1). Mono face with tabular numerals for all numerics. `--sage-dim` is never body text |

**Dependency rule:** the stack above plus PRD §24.6 and PRD §25 is the complete permitted dependency set. Anything else requires an ADR before it enters `pyproject.toml` or `package.json`.

## 4. Architecture map — the shape that must stay true

```
user graph ──emit──▶ SDK ──stamp(seq, sched_step, vclock, virtual_ts)──▶ EventWriter ──▶ SQLite (append-only)
                                                                                          │
                                                              run sealed ─────────────────┤
                                                                                          ▼
                                                                     export Parquet ──▶ DuckDB views
                                                                                          │
                                                          analysers (PURE, ordered) ◀──────┘
                                                                 │
                       findings + scorecard + verdict ──▶ SQLite (analysis tables)
                                                                 │
                                                        FastAPI ──REST/WS──▶ Control Tower
```

**Layer contract** (PRD §24.3; enforced by import-linter — this table is the config's source of truth)

| Layer | May import | Must not import |
|---|---|---|
| `events/` | — | everything (it is the root contract) |
| `store/` | `events` | `runtime`, `sdk`, `analysis` |
| `runtime/` | `events`, `store`, **`scenario`** (ratified 2026-08-17, ADR-013 — `runtime/faults/registry.py` reads the `scenario.schema` fault catalogue rather than duplicating it; one-way, `scenario/` still imports nothing) | `analysis`, `sdk` |
| `sdk/` | `events`, `runtime` | `analysis` |
| `analysis/` | `events`, `store` | **`runtime`, `sdk`, any model client** (I3, I13) |
| `explore/` | `runtime`, `analysis.race` | `store`, `sdk`, `scenario`, `otel`, `api`, `cli` (tightened 2026-09-07, op2-audit-p13.md finding #6 — `.importlinter` previously left this cell's "—" as a literal no-op, encoding only the universal api/cli rule) |
| `api/` | `store`, `analysis`, **`scenario`** (ratified 2026-09-05, ADR-023 — `routes/runs.py`/`routes/scenarios.py` resolve scenario documents for real, load-bearing reasons: `POST /api/runs`'s scenario resolution, I12 chaos-authorization itself; already true since P14 shipped, never named before) | **`runtime`** — the API server never imports the runtime; it launches runs as a **subprocess** and tails the event table (PRD §24.2) |
| `cli/` | everything | — |

Two processes share the SQLite file in WAL mode: the runner (single writer) and the API server (reader only, never writes events).

**The one thing about `events/` every later prompt must know.** The canonical projection (PRD §10.7) is **derived** from per-field `Volatility` marks in `events/schema.py`. It is not a list. **A hand-maintained set of excluded fields anywhere in this codebase is an I1 defect, not a shortcut** — including one transcribed from PRD §10.7, whose own list calls itself exhaustive and was already missing `run_end.payload.wall_makespan_ms` (§10 C-7). To change what participates in determinism equality, edit the field's mark; `canonical.py`, the validators, `docs/event-schema.md` and the property test all follow. Ask `schema.excluded_field_paths()`; never restate it.

| 6 | `runtime/` — scheduler, clock, determinism traps | P06 | 2 | P0 | BUILT — audited twice (two independent OP-2s, both **FAIL**) and repaired twice (two OP-3s); **not `VERIFIED`** | **G3** — `pytest tests/determinism/test_replay_equality.py` passes locally (3/3: 100-run replay equality incl. 10 fresh-process replays, identical schedule_decision sequence, seed-divergence — now including a real `causes=` edge, see §13); **not yet run in a real CI job** — this build has no `.git` in the transferred cloud copy (§13) | **First independent OP-2** (fresh agent, cold read, 2026-08-14) returned **FAIL** — a PRD §14.2 vector-clock defect and an `sdk.generic.Recorder` interface mismatch (§13), both root cause (a), repaired same session. A `RunHost` gap (root cause (d)) was found and left **unresolved by owner instruction** — it is not this module's repair to guess at. **Second independent OP-2** (fresh agent, cold read, 2026-09-05, `op2-audit-p06-second.md`), re-auditing the entire dispatch/concurrency fix sequence (ADR-017 through ADR-022) built since the first audit: N-way/nested fan-out via the protected `begin_call`/`spawn`/`join` path, exception propagation and `_backgrounded` staleness (ADR-022) all held; determinism (I1) confirmed byte-identical across repeated real-fixture runs. But **FAIL, 3 new findings**: (1) **CRITICAL** — `yield_point`/`sleep` had no analog of `begin_call`'s ambient-identity ownership check, so two concurrent real tasks sharing one inherited scheduler identity (e.g. two concurrent LLM calls via `asyncio.gather` within one agent step, a pattern PRD §8.8 documents as supported) silently collided, orphaning one call's `Future` and surfacing a misleading error up to 200 ticks later — demonstrated live against the real `Scheduler`, two ways. (2) **HIGH** — ADR-021's root-entry fix (`yield_point("sdk_run_entry")`) was wired into `sdk/generic.py::run()` only, with nothing equivalent for a *spawned* node body's own first dispatch — a node whose own body immediately needs several real ticks to settle (e.g. an internal subgraph invocation) reproduces the identical `DeadlockError`, one level down; also found in 9-plus of a 180-run fuzz sweep's 55 failures. (3) **MEDIUM-HIGH** — `make_run_id`'s 32-bit hash has a real, demonstrated collision rate (found in under 30 000 tries), and ADR-020's silent-reuse path never cross-checked the stored run's actual `(seed, scenario_hash, graph_hash)` before reusing its verdict — a genuine collision between different inputs would silently misattribute a stale verdict. Owner authorized fixing all three in full (`AskUserQuestion`, "Fix all three now"). **Repaired same day** (`op3-repair-report-p06-second.md`): (1) new `Scheduler._check_ambient_identity_ownership` (reusing `begin_call`'s own comparison verbatim) called from `yield_point`/`sleep`, raising `SchedulerError` immediately on a collision instead of silently corrupting `_task_futures` — the audit's own "at minimum, detect the overwrite" suggested direction, not its novel mint-inline alternative (which has no natural retirement point). (2) `sdk/langgraph.py::_run_node_body` now takes the same `yield_point("sdk_node_entry")` ADR-021 gave root, as its own first statement — verified causally by temporarily reverting the one line and reproducing the exact deadlock, then restoring it; separately confirmed the audit's own literal repro shape (a mounted subgraph) is unreachable through the shipped adapter today (`test_a_mounted_subgraph_is_a_fatal_gap_rather_than_one_opaque_agent`, `E-INSTR-002`), so the fix closes the general first-dispatch principle defensively rather than a currently-live crash. (3) new `RunIdCollisionError` (`cli/host.py`), distinct from `RunAlreadyExistsError`; `open_run` now compares `scenario_hash`/`graph_hash`/`seed` before treating a sealed collision as a legitimate rerun; `run.py` classifies a genuine collision as `INTERNAL_ERROR` (exit 5), never a fabricated pass/fail. 4 new tests (2 scheduler unit tests, 2 integration tests forcing a deterministic collision/deadlock via monkeypatch), all real-mechanism, before/after evidence. Full suite (`tests/api` excluded, D-66): 2199 passed / 1 failed (the standing, unrelated `test_doctor` D-66 gap) / 11 deselected; G1/G4/G6/G7 re-confirmed; `ruff`/`mypy --strict`/`lint-imports`/`check_determinism_hygiene.py` all clean. **A third independent re-audit is owed before `VERIFIED`**, same standing pattern as every other module |
| 7 | `runtime/cache/` — record / replay / perturb | P07 | 3 | P0 | BUILT — audited twice (two independent OP-2s, both **FAIL**) and repaired twice (two OP-3s); **not `VERIFIED`** | **G9** — offline demo not yet run against this module; 95/95 `tests/{unit,integration}/cache/` pass locally (real live run, not a proxy — see §13) | First independent OP-2 (2026-08-16) returned **FAIL** (4 findings: key material could silently key on a process memory address; `E-CACHE-002` overloaded; `response_hash` written but never verified; two tests with no real discriminating power). Repaired same session. **Second independent OP-2** (fresh agent, cold read, 2026-09-05, `op2-audit-p07-second.md`) re-audited that repair adversarially — `response_hash` verification, the `E-CACHE-012` split, and the two previously-blind tests all held. **FAIL, 4 new findings**: (1) **HIGH** — the `KeyMaterialError` guard only pattern-matched CPython's default `__repr__` address shape; a custom `__repr__` embedding the same process-local `id()` differently sailed through, demonstrated live including a genuine spurious cache-key collision between two different calls. (2) **MEDIUM** — `SqliteCacheStore._as_int` could leak a raw `ValueError` instead of `CacheStoreError` for a hand-tampered non-numeric integer column. (3) **HIGH, reconfirmed** — PRD §11.3's `CacheHook` scheduler interception point is still never called anywhere, unchanged across the entire ADR-017→ADR-022 scheduler rewrite; already disclosed, not newly broken. (4) **MEDIUM, new** — `cli/commands/run.py` called `build_cache_hook(...)` and silently discarded the result, misleadingly reading like the wiring gap had been addressed. **Repaired same day** (`op3-repair-report-p07-second.md`): (1) inverted the detection policy — the closed set of representable types is now the only set considered safe at all, replacing pattern-matching with an unconditional refusal (`_reject_unrepresentable`) for anything else. (2) `_as_int`'s string branch now catches `ValueError` and raises `CacheStoreError("E-CACHE-002", ...)`. (3) left open, disclosed — a `runtime/`-layer decision, explicitly out of this module's own scope per the audit itself, same precedent as the P06 `RunHost` gap. (4) the discarded call and its now-unused import deleted, replaced with an explanatory comment. **Notable methodology discovery this cycle**: a disclosed, never-committed stdlib compat shim (backporting `tomllib`/`datetime.UTC`/`enum.StrEnum`) plus `pip install`ing the project's real dependencies lets this sandbox run the **real, unmodified test suite** for everything except `api/` (whose PEP 695 syntax is genuinely unparseable by CPython 3.10's own parser, not merely an import gap) — `pytest --ignore=tests/api`: 2208 passed, 5 failed (the standing `test_doctor` D-66 baseline plus 4 fresh-subprocess tests whose own explicit `env=` doesn't inherit the shim, unrelated to this repair); `ruff`/`mypy --strict`/`lint-imports` all real and clean on every touched file. **A third independent re-audit is owed** — this repair is self-verified only, now with materially stronger live evidence than prior D-66-blocked cycles this session |

**Two sequencing hazards baked into this table — read before starting P11 or P15.**
- P11 bundles a hard-floor P0 item (baseline + verdict, week 6) with a cut-safe P1 item (resilience, week 9). Row 11b exists so cut 3 can be taken without unpicking a prompt. Build them as separate modules and separate commits.
- G8 is a **single** PRD gate (§44.1) covering waterfall + ghost baseline + scorecard + graph + findings + scrubber + cross-highlighting. It is therefore only satisfiable at P16, and it depends on the graph panel, which is scope-cut #4. If cut 4 is taken, G8 must be formally amended by ADR — it cannot simply be reported as passing.

Implements the complete CLI surface (`src/agentdx/cli/`, Typer, one module per command) and CI mode per §37.1/§37.2/§37.3/§22.1–22.7: `instrument` (static `resolve_graph_identity` preview, no import/execution), `run` (target/scenario resolution, real `Scheduler`/`Cache`/`EventWriter`/`FaultRegistry` construction via a new `CliRunHost` implementing the long-unimplemented `sdk.generic.RunHost` Protocol, analysis + assertion evaluation, human and `--ci` machine output, `--baseline-run` regression comparison), `doctor` (Python/LangGraph version, `PYTHONHASHSEED`, cache existence, store migration state, port conflict — six checks), `scenario {validate,list,expand}`, `version`; `replay`/`analyze`/`compare`/`export`/`import`/`scenario new`/`cache *`/`baseline update`/`bench` remain explicit exit-2 stubs, correctly out of an already-large prompt's remaining budget rather than silently missing. All 8 §37.2 exit codes each proven by a real `CliRunner`-invoked process exit status (`tests/integration/cli/test_exit_codes.py`), including a **genuine, unforced exit 5**: running any real fixture graph (`code_pipeline`, `--cache-mode replay` or `passthrough`) deterministically deadlocks, because — newly discovered this prompt — nothing in `sdk/` (`LangGraphAdapter` nor the generic `@agent`/`@tool` decorator path) ever calls `runtime.scheduler.Scheduler.spawn()`; every real agent runs as one coroutine inside the scheduler's single root task, and LangGraph's own parallel-branch fan-out (`code_pipeline`'s `coder`/`reviewer` running concurrently after `planner`) suspends on something the scheduler's cooperative loop can't observe, so it sees no runnable task and no timer and deadlocks. This blocks every fixture from completing a real run and is out of `cli/`'s own DELIVERABLES to fix (needs a separately-reviewed `sdk/`-layer design for how an agent step becomes a scheduler task) — documented in `run.py`'s and `test_exit_codes.py`'s module docstrings and in `docs/cli.md`'s own "Known gap" section, used honestly as the exit-5 test case rather than mocked away. `agentdx doctor` verified against three deliberately broken setups (wrong `PYTHONHASHSEED`, a fabricated downlevel store schema, a bound port) plus a healthy control, 4/4 passing. `--ci` mode's JUnit XML + JSON output (§22.4) and regression comparison (§22.6) are tested directly (10/10 `tests/integration/cli/test_ci_mode.py`, four added later — see **D-63**) and demonstrated end-to-end through the shipped `.github/workflows/agentdx.yml` (§22.5), run locally command-for-command — **correction (D-63): this paragraph originally read "all five PRD tolerances... tested directly (6/6)", which overstated what was proven at P17 build time. `REGRESSION_TOLERANCES` and `check_regression()` in `ci.py` were correct, and the 6 original tests did exercise all five metrics — but only against hand-constructed `ScenarioOutcome` fixtures, never through the real production pipeline. `coordination_score` specifically was never wired from a real run into that pipeline (`_finish`/`_metrics_of`/`CliRunSummary.metric()` all omitted it), so a real `--baseline-run` comparison silently skipped this metric's tolerance every time. Closed same-day by independent review; see D-63 for the full account.** `agentdx scenario validate scenarios/` exits 0 (2/2 valid); `agentdx run scenarios/ --ci --format junit --out ci-out/ --cache-mode replay` exits 5 (the documented gap above) but produces a well-formed `junit.xml` with real `<failure>` elements naming the `DeadlockError`, which is itself the honest, correct CI signal a real user would see today. **Two pre-existing bugs found and fixed in shared infrastructure while wiring `cli/` to it, neither in `cli/`'s own new code:** `CliRunHost.open_run()` built via a straight read of `sdk.generic.RunHost`'s docstring never passed `cache=` to `RunContext.create()`, silently defaulting to `NoCache()` — the real `Cache` object `build_cache()` constructs was built but never actually reachable by any SDK LLM call; fixed by threading a `cache` constructor parameter through, with a documented `cast` across the dual, independently-defined, field-identical `CachedResponse` dataclasses in `runtime.cache.store`/`sdk.generic` (a deliberate, pre-existing, already-tested cross-layer pattern, not a new gap introduced here). `_output.Output`'s `stdout`/`stderr` dataclass fields were bare `= sys.stdout`/`= sys.stderr` defaults — evaluated once at class-definition/import time, so any caller redirecting the streams after import (including `typer.testing.CliRunner`, and potentially any real embedding of the CLI as a library) silently never saw the redirect; fixed via `field(default_factory=lambda: sys.stdout)`. **One bug introduced and fixed within this same prompt, caught by its own test before being considered done:** `ci.ScenarioOutcome.as_dict()` never serialized `self.metrics` into its output dict, silently dropping metrics on every `--baseline-run` JSON round trip and defeating `check_regression` entirely (`base_value is None` on every metric, so every comparison was skipped) — `test_check_regression_detects_a_seeded_speedup_regression` failed first (`assert 0 == 1`, expected one violation, got none), diagnosed, fixed (`out["metrics"] = dict(self.metrics)` when non-empty), then passed. **Verification in full:** `mypy --strict src/` clean (98 files); `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean across all of `cli/` and its tests (one unrelated, pre-existing unformatted file elsewhere in the tree, `tests/api/test_runs.py`, untouched by this prompt, not fixed here to keep the diff scoped); `lint-imports` 10/10 contracts kept (149 files, 730 dependencies), including `cli/ may import everything` and every other layer's own restriction unchanged; whole-repository `pytest -q` (not just `tests/integration/cli/`) passes in full under `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`, confirming nothing outside `cli/` regressed; `tests/integration/cli/` itself: 22/22 (`test_exit_codes.py` 8, `test_doctor.py` 4, `test_ci_mode.py` 6, `test_scenario_commands.py` 4).

**Active prompt (superseded by the above; kept as history, not deleted):** **P16 — `BUILT`, self-reported only; no OP-2 audit yet — the same standing pattern every module in this ledger carries at this stage.** Full account in §13's new P16 row (2026-08-24); summarised here. Builds the Graph (React Flow, deterministic layered layout — **D-59**, dagre deviation), Findings, Chaos and Timeline panels, the WebSocket live stream (`eventsSlice.ts` — exponential-backoff reconnect resuming at `maxSeqSeen + 1`, degrade-to-polling with a visible `wsStatus` badge, PRD §26.2), cross-panel linking (`linking.ts`, evidence-shape ruling **C-32**), and state reconstruction at an arbitrary virtual timestamp (the Timeline scrubber against the real `GET /api/runs/{id}/state`/`state_at`, FR-10). **Four real bugs found and fixed, none by an external audit:** ① `Graph.tsx`'s custom edge component never called `getBezierPath` — React Flow's custom-edge props don't supply `labelX`/`labelY` directly — so no edge ever rendered visually; fixed by computing the path and rendering via `BaseEdge`/`EdgeLabelRenderer`. ② A genuine backend evidence/rendering gap: a finding's evidence `span_ids` can name a span nested *outside* what `get_waterfall` actually renders (an outer per-agent-turn span vs. the inner tool-call spans the waterfall shows), so `crossHighlight.spec.ts` (this prompt's own literal G8 gate test) initially failed with the target span simply absent from the DOM — fixed in `gen/make_fixtures.py` (nearest-leaf-span resolution, `_nearest_leaf_span`), declared as a real, documented gap between `analysis.race`'s evidence and `get_waterfall`'s rendered span set, explicitly out of P16's own "no new analysis" scope to fix at the root. ③ `Chaos.tsx`/`Timeline.tsx` read `run?.run_id` (server-echoed) instead of `runSlice.runId` (the route's own id) for their run-scoped network calls — harmless in production where the two always agree, but a real bug class (a misbehaving backend, or any test fixture where they diverge, silently calls the wrong URL) — fixed to read `runSlice.runId` uniformly. ④ **The most severe, found via the full e2e suite regressing:** `graphSlice`/`findingsSlice`/`chaosSlice`'s `loadGraph`/`loadFindings`/`loadScenario`/`fireStaged` all trusted `!result.error` alone as their success guard, but `openapi-fetch` can return `{data: undefined, error: undefined}` on a non-JSON error body (this build's Vite dev-proxy 500 page when the backend is unreachable) — `graph: undefined` then reached `GraphPanel`'s `graph === null` guard (`undefined !== null`), throwing and unmounting the **entire route**, taking P15's previously-working Waterfall/Scorecard down with it; fixed by treating `result.data === undefined` as an error too in all three slices, plus a new `PanelErrorBoundary` wrapping every one of `RunRoute.tsx`'s five panels so a future render-time crash in any one panel is contained to its own grid cell (**D-60**, not in P16's own `DELIVERABLES`, declared). **FR-10 (state reconstruction, <200ms p95) measured, not assumed:** `bench/harness/scrub_reconstruction.py` samples random virtual timestamps against `SnapshottingStore`-seeded logs at both PRD §20.4's literal 5 000-event size and a 50 000-event headroom size, both comfortably under both the PRD's 100ms and this prompt's own 200ms thresholds `[bench:scrub-reconstruction.json]`. **Correction (2026-08-29, D-68): this paragraph originally quoted fixed figures here ("p95 9.1ms and 10.8ms respectively") that do not match the committed `bench/results/scrub-reconstruction.json` and were never reconciled by the P18/19 session that later touched the same file. Re-running the harness three times in immediate succession on one machine (2026-08-29) produced three different p95 values (5.73/8.58, 5.73/8.18, 5.76/9.28 ms) — this is a real wall-clock benchmark, not a fixed constant, and no single digit should be quoted here as a permanent fact. See D-68 for the full account; cite the committed file's current numbers directly instead of this paragraph's stale ones.** **Verification in full:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `eslint .` clean (0 errors, 5 pre-existing-pattern warnings, none new); 45/45 Vitest unit tests. **Correction (2026-09-07, op2-audit-p16.md finding #6): this paragraph originally claimed "no new unit tests written this session for `linking.ts`/`graphLayout.ts`/`scenario.ts`" — false for `linking.ts`: `tests/frontend/unit/linking.test.ts` (12 tests, real, passing) was committed the same session, `d12b763`. The gap was, and remains, real only for `graphLayout.ts` and `api/scenario.ts`.** `npm run test:e2e` 23/24 — `crossHighlight.spec.ts` (G8's own literal cross-panel-highlight assertion), `panels.spec.ts`, `chaos.spec.ts`, `wsReconnect.spec.ts` and every pre-existing P15 spec all pass; the sole failure, `perf.spec.ts` (NFR-3), was A/B-tested directly against unmodified P15 Waterfall-only (no P16 panels mounted) in this same sandbox and measured the identical 51.8–57.6fps range, confirming the shortfall is pre-existing sandbox noise (same class as Q-P15.1, since resolved on real hardware), not a P16 regression. **A second, initially-flaky test fixed at its root cause, not papered over:** `wsReconnect.spec.ts` intermittently never observed the `open → reconnecting → open` transition — traced to the mock server's fixed 150ms close timer racing the page's own dev-mode bootstrap (a slow first render could mean the test's own first `toHaveAttribute('...', 'open')` check matched the *second*, permanent connection instead of the first, leaving no window in which "reconnecting" could ever be observed) — fixed by keying the close to the client's actual `subscribe` message rather than wall-clock time, and by recording the full status history via a `MutationObserver` (installed before navigation) instead of sampling the DOM at discrete polling intervals, which cannot miss a transition the way interval-based `expect().toHaveAttribute()` polling can; confirmed stable over 8 consecutive runs after the fix, 3 of 8 still failing under the interim (subscribe-keyed-close-only) fix before the `MutationObserver` change.

**Next prompt:** **P18 — test hardening, false-positive suite, benchmarks** is next per the roadmap (§5); it also owns the DoD's "re-audit is owed" backlog rather than any single new module.
**Blocked on:** nothing blocks P18 from starting. Several things remain genuinely open, none blocking: **an OP-2 audit is owed for P14, P15, P16 and now P17** (self-reported only, same standing pattern as every module since P02); **the newly-discovered `sdk/`-spawn gap (this row) blocks every fixture graph from completing a real run** — it is the single highest-severity open item in this ledger, since it means `agentdx run` cannot yet produce a real pass/fail signal against any of the three reference fixtures, only the honest exit-5 deadlock; closing it needs its own reviewed design (how an agent step becomes a scheduler task under `Scheduler.spawn()`) and is explicitly not attempted here; **`replay`/`analyze`/`compare`/`export`/`import`/`scenario new`/`cache *`/`baseline update`/`bench`, and `run`'s `--baseline`/`--jobs`/`--fail-on`/`--format github`, remain stubs or no-ops** — deferred rather than cut, no §12 scope-cut order invoked, each says so plainly at the CLI (exit 2, or an explicit "not yet implemented" warning); **golden-corpora regeneration for the three fixtures against a real `Scheduler`** (`host.py`'s own prior-session note, still open, now additionally blocked on the `sdk/`-spawn gap above — a real run cannot complete to regenerate against yet); **the `RunState.ANALYSING → COMPLETE` scheduler transition** has no public setter and nothing reads for `COMPLETE` specifically — minor, non-blocking, noted here so it isn't rediscovered as new; one pre-existing, unrelated formatting drift, `tests/api/test_runs.py` (`ruff format --check` flags it; not touched by P17, not this prompt's file to fix). Carried over, unrelated to P17: **D-59**/**D-60**/**D-61** (§9, all still open from P16); the span-nesting evidence gap in `gen/make_fixtures.py` (P16, ③); `axe.spec.ts` per-panel coverage gap (P16; a test was added 2026-09-07 per op2-audit-p16.md finding #5, but is unexecuted in this sandbox — see §5 row 16); **Q-P15.1** (resolved 2026-08-24); P12's older **C-26**/**D-53**/the `analysis/timing.py:705` unverified flag.

## 8. Decision log — APPEND ONLY

Format: `ADR-NNN · date · decision · rationale · PRD sections overridden · consequences`.
Never edit or delete a row. To reverse a decision, append a higher-numbered ADR that supersedes it. Enforced by the CI check in `AGENTS.md` §10.

| ID | Date | Decision | Rationale | Overrides | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-000 | 2026-08-08 | PRD v2.0 adopted as the spec of record | Single source of truth for the build | — | All prompts cite PRD sections; the PRD is read-only in `docs/` |
| ADR-001 | 2026-08-10 | **All three reference fixtures (`code_pipeline`, `support_triage`, `research_fanout`) are built in week 1 under P05**, rather than weeks 1 / 7 / 10 | (a) `research_fanout` is the false-positive control for I4/G2. Under the PRD schedule it does not exist until week 7, so every analyser shipped in weeks 5–6 would be developed with no known-zero-findings input to test against — the exact condition that produces a detector nobody trusts. (b) The event schema freezes at the end of week 1 (43.1.5). Fixtures are the only realistic consumers that can prove the schema is sufficient *before* the freeze; a fixture built in week 10 that needs a new field forces a post-freeze schema break (tripwire 6). (c) The redundancy detector lands in week 5, four weeks before `support_triage`, its only seeded-redundancy input | PRD §40.1 (wk 7 "research fan-out fixture", wk 10 "support-triage fixture"); PRD §44.2 FR-12 "Ships in Wk 1/7/10" | 1. P05 grows and sits on the project's own critical path (§40.2) — week-1 slippage costs a week downstream. 2. Week-1 fixtures run **without** the scheduler (wk 2) or the cache (wk 3), so their golden corpora are provisional; they **must** be regenerated at the end of P07 and that regeneration is an explicit deliverable of P07, not a convenience (`AGENTS.md` §5). 3. Building `support_triage` early does **not** promote it out of the scope-cut order — it remains cut #6 (§12). 4. G1/G2/G5 expectations for all three fixtures become writable in week 1 as failing tests |
| ADR-002 | 2026-08-10 | **The k=2 false-positive harness is split out of FR-6 and made P0.** A minimal, test-only schedule enumerator lands in `tests/false_positives/` at P12 (week 7). It enumerates the k=2 frontier and asserts an empty finding set; it has no CLI, no API field, no UI, no reduction reporting and no coverage panel. FR-6 remains the shipped exploration *product* — P1, week 10, scope-cut #1 | G2 is on the never-waived list (PRD §44.3.1) yet half of it depended on FR-6, the first thing to be cut. That is a gate that can be legally deleted by a schedule slip. The dependency is also accidental rather than essential: G2 needs schedule *enumeration*, roughly a day of code, while FR-6 is enumeration plus reduction, dedup, budgeting, reporting and a UI panel. Splitting them makes G2 genuinely unwaivable at a fraction of FR-6's cost. It also matters which half survives — the 100 replays all execute the *same* interleaving, so the frontier is the half that actually tests for false positives across differing schedules | PRD §40.1 (wk 10 "Bounded exploration (FR-6)") and §44.2 FR-6 "Ships in Wk 10", **partially**: the test-only harness moves to week 7. FR-6 itself is unmoved | 1. P12 grows by the harness; write it in the same prompt as the race detector so the detector is never developed without its control. 2. Taking cut 1 no longer touches G2 — §12 updated accordingly. 3. The harness is **not** a head start on FR-6 and must not accrete features; if it grows a flag or an output format, that is scope creep to surface, not progress. 4. Supersedes the C-3 ruling in §10 |
| ADR-003 | 2026-08-10 | **LangGraph pinned to `>=1.2,<1.3`; graph API only — no `@entrypoint` / functional API in v1** | Closes Q-43.2.1, whose deadline is week 1. 1.2 is the current minor and the one the adapter will be developed and tested against; a floating pin means a LangGraph patch release can change node-callback ordering, which is an I1 failure that looks like our bug. Excluding `@entrypoint` follows the PRD §43.2 default: the graph API makes structure explicit, which is what the SDK captures, and the functional API would need a second capture path for a v1 feature nobody has asked for | — (resolves open question Q-43.2.1) | 1. `sdk/langgraph.py` targets 1.2 only; a 1.3 bump is an ADR plus a re-run of the determinism suite. 2. FastAPI does not serve itself; §12 YAML needs a parser; §8.5 explicitly rejects a vendor SDK in favour of the OpenAI-compatible surface, which needs an HTTP client. Enumerating them once is cheaper than four half-decisions in P08/P14. Owner-approved at P01 before `pyproject.toml` was written | — (interprets §3, does not override it) | 1. MIT is the lowest-friction choice for a tool whose primary evaluator (PRD §3.6) assesses it by cloning it | — | Contributions are inbound-MIT; changing this after publication requires contributor agreement, so it is effectively one-way |
| ADR-006 | 2026-08-10 | **The determinism-hygiene gate requires BOTH an allowlisted path AND a per-line `# determinism-exempt: <reason>` comment** | The P01 prompt exempted only `runtime/determinism.py`; AGENTS.md §4.1 sanctions four exceptions, and AGENTS.md wins on process (§0.5). Requiring both conditions is stricter than either source alone: a comment cannot exempt a file, a path cannot exempt a line, and an annotation on a non-allowlisted path is reported as a violation in its own right — so the escape hatch is always visible in a diff | — (implements AGENTS.md §4.1) | 1. Widening the allowlist is a reviewable edit to `scripts/check_determinism_hygiene.py` with a §4.1 clause quoted beside it. 2. `events/writer.py` and the `api/`, `cli/`, `store/` prefixes are pre-listed for clauses 3 and 4; those files do not exist yet, so the entries are inert until P02–P14 |
| ADR-007 | 2026-08-10 | **Floats are forbidden everywhere in the event log**, including inside the open sub-objects `span_start.attributes`, `fault_injected.params` and `run_start.payload.env`. Emitting one is `E-EVENT-013`. The restriction is encoded in the type system: `schema.PayloadValue` has no `float` member | The canonical projection cannot normalise cross-platform float formatting away, and PRD §10.6 already concedes floating-point results are not guaranteed across architectures — so a float in a canonical field is an I1 leak that no amount of care in `canonical.py` can close. Every quantity PRD §9.5 actually specifies is an integer, a hash string or an enum, so the rule costs nothing in the specified schema. It is an SDK-visible constraint, which is why it was raised before implementation rather than assumed. Owner-delegated at P02 | — (PRD is silent; this decides it) | 1. Durations are integer milliseconds and ratios integer per-mille, project-wide. 2. **PRD §12.2 gives the P1 fault `agent_slow` a `factor` of type `float ≥ 1.0`. When FR-4's P1 set lands it must carry `factor_milli: int`, or this ADR needs superseding.** 3. A user attaching a float to `@agentdx.span(attributes=…)` gets a hard error, not a silent coercion — P04 must say so in the SDK docs |
| ADR-008 | 2026-08-11 | **`.agentdx` bundles are a zip archive whose members are stored uncompressed inside a DEFLATE container; the `.zst` member names of PRD §20.7 are not used and `zstandard` is not adopted** | §20.7 names `events.jsonl.zst` and `cache/entries.jsonl.zst`. Zstd requires the `zstandard` distribution, which is outside the set ADR-004 enumerated, and AGENTS.md §2 requires an ADR before any new dependency enters `pyproject.toml`. The zip container already compresses, so zstd inside it is double compression for no benefit; avoiding the dependency keeps `uv pip install agentdx` compiler-free (NFR-16) and the demo dependency-light. `manifest.json` carries a `compression` field so a future zstd bundle is distinguishable rather than merely different. Owner-approved before any bundle code was written | PRD §20.7 (member *filenames* only — the zip container, the member set and the semantics are unchanged) | 1. `BUNDLE_FORMAT_VERSION = 1` covers this layout; adding, removing or renaming a member bumps it. 2. Q-43.2.8 is answered (§10). 3. Adopting zstd later is an ADR plus a format bump, never a silent filename change |
| ADR-009 | 2026-08-11 | **The `events` table gains a `schema_version INTEGER NOT NULL` column, which PRD §27.2's DDL omits** | The P02 event model marks `schema_version` `Volatility.STABLE`, so it is inside the canonical projection and therefore inside every event's `this_hash`. A row written without it cannot round-trip to the event that produced its hash, and a stored log could not re-verify its own chain. Both alternatives were worse: reconstructing the value from `runs.schema_version` makes each event's canonical hash depend on a second table being present and correct — which it is not during a bundle import, where events land before run metadata is trusted — and dropping the field from the projection is a schema change P03 had no authority to make (tripwire 6). Cost is two bytes per row. Raised as a STOP CONDITION and owner-ruled before any store code was written | PRD §27.2 (`events` DDL — **additive**; nothing was removed) | 1. `store/sqlite.py` reads and writes the column and the Parquet export carries it, so the SQLite and DuckDB paths agree. 2. `tests/unit/store/test_round_trip.py::test_schema_version_survives_the_round_trip` asserts it exists and round-trips. 3. If PRD §27.2 is ever revised, this column is the revision |
| ADR-010 | 2026-08-11 | **`.agentdx` bundle format 2: the archive gains an `events.chain` member — one `this_hash` per event, in seq order — and it is a required member** | PRD §36 specifies the `E-BUNDLE-001` user message as *"Bundle integrity check failed at event 1043"*, i.e. it must name the failing event. Format 1 carried only the rolling canonical log hash and the final chain head, from which a divergence can be **detected** but never **located** — a rolling hash says that something moved, not where. An OP-2 probe altered `sched_step` on seq 21 of a 73-event log: `verify` reported the mismatch and returned `first_bad_seq = None`, so the specified message was undeliverable. With the per-event chain, `canonical.verify_chain` returns 21 exactly. Cost is roughly 80 bytes per event inside a DEFLATE container | PRD §20.7 (member list — **additive**) | 1. `BUNDLE_FORMAT_VERSION` is 2; format 1 bundles are not produced by this build and are refused as unlocatable. 2. `verify` now reports both *that* and *where*. 3. Any future member change bumps the format again |

Two of the flagged entries were additionally mis-filed under the wrong kind of gap: `code_pipeline`'s coordination-bottleneck finding and `support_triage`'s fake-fan-out distinction are not unmeasured, the code that would produce either signal does not exist in `graph.py` at all — a timing model at P07 would not help either one, which a single `computable_from_this_log: false` flag could not distinguish from the genuinely timing-dependent findings sitting right next to them | — (implements I6 / tripwire 4 for fixture data; no PRD section specifies this schema, so nothing is overridden) | 1. All three fixtures' `golden_findings.json` rewritten under this schema, same day (§13). 2. `scripts/check_fixture_finding_evidence.py` enforces it going forward — scans only `findings`, fails on any empty or missing `evidence.seq` — wired into `just fixtures-check`. 3. A fourth array or a boolean flag on a `findings` entry reintroduces exactly the defect this ADR closes; a future fixture's `golden_findings.json` must use these three arrays and no others |

Owner-approved default ("keep as a declared, provisional exception"), applied 2026-08-13 | — (clarifies, rather than overrides, ADR-001 consequence 2; see C-10 for the tripwire-11 ruling this ADR rests on) | 1. `fixtures/_harness.py`'s module docstring was corrected in the same repair pass: its earlier claim of clean footing under `AGENTS.md` §4.1 clause 4 was a stretch — the module's entire job is running things inside a run context, the opposite of clause 4's condition — and it now says so, naming clause 3 (the volatile-field writer) as the nearer analogy instead of clause 4. 2.
