| **Q-P02.1** | **PRD §9.5 specifies payloads for 9 of the 19 event types. The other 10 were derived at P02 — do they stand?** | Adopt the P02 derivation; every field cites its source and is marked `derived=True` in `schema.py` | Week 1 — before the freeze | **Accepted 2026-08-10 with three amendments** (see below). ⚠️ **Accepted on owner delegation, not independent review** — the same agent derived and accepted these schemas. Re-examining them is the highest-value thing an OP-2 audit of `events/` can do |
| **Q-P15.1** | **NFR-3 (60fps to 50 agents/5 000 spans) — real, current measurement in this build's cloud sandbox** | N/A — a measured fact, not a design default | Before P16 (or whenever the perf gate runs in a real CI environment) | **RESOLVED 2026-08-24** (see full account at row end). Eight consecutive real Playwright-trace runs of `tests/frontend/e2e/perf.spec.ts` against a synthetic 5 280-span/24-lane fixture measured **58.2–60.3fps** — clustered right at the 60fps line, not comfortably above it, in this shared-vCPU, GPU-less headless sandbox. Two real fixes already took the same measurement from 6.1fps → 32.2fps → ~59fps (`frontend/src/panels/spanWindow.ts`'s binary search replacing an O(spans)-per-lane-per-scroll-frame filter; then event delegation + a plain `<rect>` replacing `@visx/shape`'s `<Bar>` + dropping a redundant per-span `<title>` in `Waterfall.tsx`'s `LaneRow` — both files' comments have the full account). The remaining gap was **not** closed by loosening the test's `>= 60` assertion (AGENTS.md §5: never weaken a test to pass) — it stays failing intermittently in this sandbox by design, so the real number is what's on record, not a manufactured green. **Needs a real (non-shared, GPU-accelerated) CI runner to get a trustworthy pass/fail signal**; whoever runs that owns closing this row — **CLOSED 2026-08-24**: the same, unmodified `perf.spec.ts` run via `npm run test:e2e` on the project owner's own machine (real, non-shared, GPU-accelerated hardware, not this build's cloud sandbox) passed cleanly — 15/15 e2e specs including this one, 11.0s total. The >=60fps NFR-3 floor holds under real conditions exactly as this row predicted; the 58.2-60.3fps sandbox measurement was accurately-reported sandbox noise, not a masked defect |

**Q-P02.1 amendments, applied 2026-08-10.** ① `lock_acquire.contended` **removed** — under the cooperative single-threaded scheduler (PRD §10.2) it is exactly `wait_virtual_ms > 0`, so it was a second source of truth for one fact, and a schema that can express `contended=false, wait_virtual_ms=50` is worse than one that cannot. `wait_virtual_ms` stays: the log records only the grant, never the attempt, so wait time is not otherwise derivable and PRD §16.2's coordination bucket needs it. ② `barrier.participants` and `schedule_decision.ready_task_ids` marked **`set_valued`**, with new code **`E-EVENT-028`** rejecting unsorted emission at write time — canonicalisation still refuses to reorder (that would hide a nondeterministic emitter), but the emitter now fails loudly and correctly attributed instead of surfacing as an intermittent G3 failure in week 2. ③ `nondeterminism_warning.source` **opened from a closed enum to a free string** — that event type exists to record surprises, and a closed enum would turn an unanticipated leak into `E-EVENT-005` and abort the run at the exact moment the system was trying to report something useful.

**The freeze is no longer blocked by Q-P02.1.** It remains blocked by the P05 fixtures (ADR-001: fixtures are the only realistic consumers that can prove the schema *sufficient* before the freeze) and by a green `just ci` on Python 3.12. No other open question blocks week-1 work.

**Known PRD-internal conflicts and their rulings.** Recorded so they are not re-litigated every session. A ruling here is binding until an ADR changes it.

| ID | Conflict | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | §40.1 (wk 5) says overhead buckets "sum to **wall clock** ±2%" and §44.1 G5 says "makespan" unqualified; §16.2.3 computes against `virtual_makespan` | **§16.2.3 is normative.** The quantity is *virtual* makespan, per I11. §40.1's "wall clock" is loose roadmap phrasing. Also note the shape: it is Σ(six buckets) + `residual` = makespan with residual < 2 %, not "buckets + critical path" — the critical path *is* what the buckets decompose |
| C-2 | §4.3 and §7 define **FR-13 = one-page HTML/PDF report export (P2)**; §44.2 lists **FR-13 = OTel export, Wk 10** | Treat them as two items: **FR-13 = report export (P2, not in v1)**; **OTel export = an unnumbered P1 item, week 10, scope-cut #5** (per §4.2 and §40.3). §44.2's label is a typo. Do not build a report exporter in v1. **Confirmed by the human owner, 2026-08-10 — closed, do not re-litigate** |
| C-3 | G2 (never-waived, §44.3) requires a clean k=2 exploration frontier, but exploration is FR-6 — P1 and scope-cut #1 | **Resolved by ADR-002:** the k=2 harness is split out of FR-6 and made P0 at P12. Both halves of G2 are now unwaivable and no scope cut reaches them. Closed |
| C-4 | §9.7 requires a `prev_hash`/`this_hash` chain and §38's DDL has both as `events` **columns**, but §9.2's canonical event schema lists neither field | **§38 is normative: the chain lives beside the event, never inside it.** Forced rather than chosen — an event whose canonical form contained its own hash is self-referential. The chain covers the canonical projection only, or every run gets a unique chain and bundle tamper-detection becomes useless. `docs/event-schema.md` §6, §11 C-4 |
| C-5 | §9.2 marks `run_id` volatile=`no` and §10.7 says "every other field participates in equality", which puts it in the projection; §6.1 defines it as `r_` + 5 hex of *a content hash* of unstated inputs | **`run_id` is `identity` — excluded from the projection.** Both literal readings break something: any per-execution component in the hash means no two replays agree and **G3 can never reach 100/100**; a pure content hash means §33.3's 100 replays collide on `runs.run_id PRIMARY KEY` (§38). A third volatility mark exists precisely because `run_id` is neither volatile nor stable. Covers `llm_call.payload.perturbed_from_run`, which is a run id. `docs/event-schema.md` §11 R-1 |
| C-6 | §10.7's code pops `payload.cache_key`; §10.7's prose exclusion list — which calls itself exhaustive — does not list it; §11.4 states the key never contains a machine-local salt | **`cache_key` is stable and stays in the projection.** Three of four sources agree; the popping line carries a comment that asks and answers its own question. Excluding it would hide a genuine prompt divergence — the silent-pass failure, which is worse than an unpassable gate. `docs/event-schema.md` §11 R-2 |
| C-7 | §9.3 gives `run_end` a wall makespan; §10.7's exclusion list calls itself exhaustive and omits it | **`run_end.payload.wall_makespan_ms` is volatile and excluded.** Left as specified it is the second field that would have made G3 unpassable. Found at P02 by deriving `run_end`'s payload. **Mitigation adopted: the exclusion list is now generated from the schema marks (`schema.excluded_field_paths()`) and asserted against the docs by a test, so this class of gap cannot recur.** `docs/event-schema.md` §11 R-3 |
| C-8 | §9.8 says the vclock must be "≥ previous vclock for the slot"; §14.2's rules increment on every local event, implying strict `>` | **§9.8 governs the validator (`E-EVENT-027` fires on `<` only).** It is the validation section, and the looser rule cannot produce a false rejection whereas the stricter one could. `docs/event-schema.md` §11 R-5 |
| C-9 | §11.2 defines a `passthrough` cache mode that bypasses the cache entirely, but §9.5's `llm_call.payload.cache_status` enum has four members — `hit`, `miss_recorded`, `miss_error`, `perturbed` — and none of them means "the cache was bypassed" | **The shim reports `miss_recorded` and `run_start.payload.cache_mode` is what distinguishes a passthrough run from a record run.** Adding a fifth member is a schema change and needs an ADR plus a `schema_version` bump (tripwire 6), which P04 has no authority to take — so it is surfaced here rather than decided. Raised **before** the freeze deliberately: after it, the cost is every recorded run. `docs/sdk.md` §5, §10 |
| C-10 | Does `fixtures/_harness.py` — not named in P05's `DELIVERABLES` — violate tripwire 11 ("a prompt's output covers modules its `DELIVERABLES` list did not name")? | **No.** ADR-012 rules it a necessary consequence of ADR-001's own commitment (running all three fixtures end to end in week 1, before `runtime/` or `runtime/cache/` exist), not an unscoped addition. The distinguishing test applied: does this module do anything a real runtime component would still need to do *differently*, or does it only supply the minimum stamping `DraftEvent → Event` needs to become a valid, writable log? It is the latter — see `fixtures/_harness.py`'s own docstring for exactly what is honestly synthetic (`sched_step`, `virtual_ts_ms`) versus real, exercised code (`EventWriter`, `Store`, the vector-clock construction). Tripwire 11 remains live for anything built beyond that boundary — a scheduler feature, a second cache mode, or anything this module grows that a real fixture run does not strictly need |
| C-11 | §21.2's `[SOURCE]` worked example has neither a `version:` nor a `target:` key and is presented as a scenario that "must still validate"; §21.3's validation-rules table, read literally, could be taken to mean a missing `version` is an error the way an unsupported explicit value is | **§21.2's worked example is normative for the missing-key case.** `version` absent defaults to `1` (not an error); `target` absent is resolved by inferring `{fixture: <name>}` from a `task` path shaped like `fixtures/<name>/...`, exactly as §21.2's own note describes. §21.3's table covers only an *explicit and wrong* value (`version: 2` on a build that supports only `1`) — it does not say a missing key is an error, and reading it that way would make the PRD's own canonical example fail its own schema, which is the shape STOP CONDITION 3 exists to catch. Verified: the exact §21.2 text validates with 0 errors under this ruling (`test_prd_21_2_source_scenario_validates_unchanged`) | E-SCEN-001 (explicit wrong version only); `docs/scenario-reference.md` §Versioning |
| C-12 | §21.5's matrix example, `matrix: {seed: [1,2,3], fault_type: [agent_crash, tool_failure]}`, uses a bare `fault_type` key that does not correspond to any top-level scenario field — a fault's type lives at `faults[i].type`, and neither §21.1 nor §21.5 says which `i` a bare `fault_type` key should mean | **No PRD text resolves this — it is a genuine, load-bearing gap, ruled rather than guessed at.** A matrix key is either one of three bare aliases (`seed`, `mode`, `repeats` — the top-level scalars unambiguous enough not to need a path) or a dotted/bracketed path into the document, identical in shape to the `ScenarioPath` `validate.py` already reports errors against (`faults[0].type`, `guards.max_tokens`). §21.5's own `fault_type` example must be rewritten `"faults[0].type"` under this scheme; a bare key that is neither an alias nor a valid path is rejected with an error naming this convention explicitly, never silently guessing which fault index was meant | `matrix.py`'s module docstring; `docs/scenario-reference.md` §Matrix expansion. **Flagged for a PRD amendment** — propose §21.5's example be rewritten to the dotted-path form at the next revision, since as written it cannot be typed into a real scenario file and validate |
| C-13 | An independent OP-2 audit (2026-08-16) proved C-11's inference rule, as shipped, resolves PRD §21.2's own literal `task: fixtures/tasks/refactor_module.md` to the bogus `target.fixture: "tasks"` with zero validation errors — `fixtures/tasks/` is a directory shared across fixtures (referenced by `code_pipeline`, `research_fanout`, `support_triage` alike), not itself one, but it is syntactically `fixtures/<name>/...`-shaped, so the naive inference rule cannot tell the two apart. Genuine spec gap: nothing in §21.2 says how a shared, non-fixture task directory should be handled | **`fixtures/tasks/` (and any future shared, non-fixture directory under `fixtures/`) is not inferable — `infer_fixture_from_task_path` returns `None` for it, and the scenario must set `target:` explicitly**, exactly as §21.2's own note allows ("... or supplied by `--fixture`"). PRD §21.2's literal `[SOURCE]` text therefore does **not** validate with zero errors as literally written — it needs an explicit `target: {fixture: code_pipeline}` added (the fixture `kill_reviewer.yaml`, the sibling scenario targeting the same `reviewer` agent, already lives in). This narrows C-11, which remains correct for its own actual scope (an *explicit*-target document needs no `version` correction) but whose "Verified" citation was false — see the amendment note below the table | `loader.infer_fixture_from_task_path`/`non_fixture_task_dir`, `E-SCEN-003`; `docs/scenario-reference.md` §`target`; `scenarios/reviewer_crash_midflight.yaml` (corrected); `tests/unit/scenario/test_validate.py::test_prd_21_2_source_scenario_literal_text_requires_explicit_target` |
| C-14 | The same OP-2 audit found `tool_failure`'s trigger vocabulary (`schema.py`'s `FAULT_CATALOGUE`) was never cross-checked against PRD §12.2/§12.3 at all — build-time STOP CONDITION 1 covered `agent_crash` only. §12.2's column reads `always \| first_n \| after_virtual_ts \| probability`; §12.3's canonical `should_fire` pseudocode has no `first_n` case at all, and its `AtVirtualTs` case is `after_virtual_ts` under a different name. The shipped code had silently substituted `AFTER_N_MESSAGES` (a Transport-class, edge-message concept) for both, with no clear meaning for a tool-targeted fault and no ruling logged | **`tool_failure.trigger_kinds` becomes `(ALWAYS, AT_VIRTUAL_TS, PROBABILITY)`** — `AT_VIRTUAL_TS` is the unambiguous match for `after_virtual_ts`; `first_n` has no §12.3 equivalent and is not supported (flagged for a PRD amendment, same as C-12). `AFTER_N_MESSAGES` is removed from `tool_failure`'s vocabulary | `schema.FAULT_CATALOGUE["tool_failure"]`; `docs/scenario-reference.md` §Fault catalogue; `tests/unit/scenario/test_validate.py`'s `test_tool_failure_accepts_at_virtual_ts_trigger`/`test_tool_failure_rejects_after_n_messages_trigger` |
| C-15 | A second independent OP-2 audit (2026-08-16, re-auditing the first OP-3 repair) proved two more gaps: (1) the first C-13 fix denylisted only the single name `"tasks"` and still silently mis-inferred `fixtures/perturbations/...` (a second real, shipped, non-fixture directory) to a bogus `target.fixture`, reproduced live; (2) neither an inferred nor an explicit `target.fixture` naming a nonexistent fixture (typo, or wholly fictional) was ever checked at validation time at all, contradicting PRD §21.3's own closing rationale ("failing after a 40-second run because of a typo is unacceptable in CI") | **(1) `infer_fixture_from_task_path`/`non_fixture_task_dir` now check `fixtures/<name>/graph.py` actually exists on disk, replacing the per-name denylist — this generalises to any future non-fixture directory without a name being hand-enumerated (this is C-13's correction, not a new ruling in its own right — see the amendment note below the table). (2) New `E-SCEN-012`: when `target.fixture` is set (inferred or explicit) and the repo root is resolvable, `fixtures/<fixture>/graph.py` must exist, or it's an error. Deliberately scoped to `fixture` only — `target.graph` remains best-effort (a legitimate user graph may not resolve statically, and that is not an error), since fixtures, unlike user graphs, are a closed, project-owned set** | `loader._is_real_fixture`/`infer_fixture_from_task_path`/`non_fixture_task_dir`; `validate._check_target_fixture_exists`, `E-SCEN-012`; `docs/scenario-reference.md` §`target`, §Error codes; `tests/unit/scenario/test_loader.py`'s parametrised `test_infer_fixture_from_task_path_declines_every_non_fixture_directory`/`test_infer_fixture_from_task_path_accepts_every_real_fixture`; `tests/unit/scenario/test_validate.py`'s `test_explicit_target_fixture_that_does_not_exist_is_rejected`/`test_explicit_target_fixture_that_exists_is_accepted`/`test_target_graph_is_never_checked_for_fixture_existence` |
| C-16 | PRD §12.3's canonical `should_fire` pseudocode reads `fault.fired`/`fault.repeating`, but the scenario schema (PRD §21) declares no `repeating:` field anywhere a fault can set it — `fault.repeating` has no concrete definition anywhere in the PRD. Found at P09 build time (`runtime/faults/triggers.py`'s own module docstring), re-surfaced and confirmed still unresolved by the P09 OP-2 audit and put to the owner as Q4 rather than left as an inline ruling only a source-reading AI would ever see | **`REPEATING_TRIGGER_KINDS = {PROBABILITY, ALWAYS}`** — `fault.repeating` is `True` for exactly these two `TriggerKind`s, `False` for the other four (`AT_VIRTUAL_TS`, `AT_SPAN_N`, `AFTER_N_MESSAGES`, `ON_STATE_WRITE`). Rationale: `ALWAYS`/`PROBABILITY` triggers are semantically re-evaluated on every interception by design — PRD §12.2's catalogue requires an `always`-triggered `latency`/`message_drop`/`tool_failure` to affect *every* delivery/call, and `PROBABILITY` is definitionally an independent draw each time (PRD §12.5's own example: "a probabilistic drop that fires four times produces four [fault_effect events]" only makes sense if the fault keeps firing). The other four trigger kinds each describe one point in the run reached once — without one-shot gating, `AT_VIRTUAL_TS` would fire on every interception evaluated after its timestamp passes, which PRD §12.2's own single-crash semantics for `agent_crash` rules out | `runtime/faults/triggers.py`'s `REPEATING_TRIGGER_KINDS` constant and its docstring (the ruling's original location, transcribed here verbatim per the owner's Q4 answer); `docs/chaos-safety.md`; `tests/unit/faults/test_triggers.py::test_repeating_trigger_kinds_is_exactly_probability_and_always`. **Flagged for a PRD amendment** — propose either a `repeating:` scenario field or this six-line rule written into §12.3 directly, same shape as C-12 |
| C-17 | PRD §16.2.2's bucket table names `productive_work`/`orchestration` in terms of `llm_call`/`tool_call` leaf spans only. It is silent on the leftover, uncovered interval inside an `agent_step` span that `timing.py` turns into an `agent_step_segment` node — necessary because this build's golden fixtures declare `duration_virtual_ms=0` on every leaf span while still recording real elapsed time between events (see `timing.py`'s own module docstring). Found at P10 build time while wiring `overhead.py` | **An `agent_step_segment` node's duration is classified exactly as a leaf span's is: role-gated between `orchestration` (role `orchestrator`/`router`) and `productive_work` (every other role).** The more literal reading — route it to `residual` as "the PRD doesn't name it, so it's unattributed" — was tried and rejected: measured directly, it pushes all three golden fixtures' residual past gate G5's tolerance for time that is genuinely recorded, not missing. This is a judgement call on a PRD-silent point, not a forced reading — flagged here as a candidate for a future ADR if a later prompt revisits it against non-synthetic fixture data, where leaf spans carry real declared durations and this ambiguity may resolve differently | `overhead.py`'s `_classify_node` and its module docstring §"agent_step_segment bucket membership"; `docs/performance-analysis.md` §4.3 |
| C-18 | PRD §16.3's redundancy conditions require, for a reported group, "at least two members are concurrent (by vector clock) or occur within the same logical phase." "Logical phase" has no operational definition anywhere else in the 5505-line PRD — the phrase occurs exactly once, at its own definition site. Found at P10 build time while wiring `redundancy.py` | **"Same logical phase" is read as coinciding with condition 3's own same-slot, no-intervening-state-change branch** — the one case in condition 3 that does not already require concurrency to be meaningful, since same-slot events are always totally ordered, never concurrent. Under this reading a pair qualifies iff not retry-linked, and either (same slot, no intervening state change) or (different slots, concurrent). Not a forced reading — an undefined term was not guessed at, a specific, documented, testable operationalisation was chosen and surfaced, per AGENTS.md §1 | `redundancy.py`'s module docstring §"The qualifying-pair rule"; `docs/performance-analysis.md` §5.1 |
| C-19 | **D-50** (§9): `timing.py`'s `run_boundary` edges carry a non-zero, real-gap weight, contradicting PRD §16.1.1's edge table, which literally reads `0`. An independent same-session review found this changes gate G5's own pass/fail status on every golden fixture (0% residual with the code's weighting vs. 8.5%/6.3%/10.0% with the PRD's literal `0`, measured directly) and was never escalated as a `⚠️ BLOCKED` per AGENTS.md §3 rule 5, nor logged as a deviation until D-50 | **Kept as shipped — a run's own unavoidable lead-in (before its first instrumented span) and trail-off (after its last) are attributed to `blocking_wait`, not left to inflate `residual`.** Rationale: `residual` (PRD §16.2.4) is meant to flag *unexplained* elapsed time — "usually an instrumentation gap (an un-shimmed provider, an unwrapped tool, an uninstrumented subgraph)" — and a run's boundary gap is not that; it is measured, not missing (I9: the gap *is* measured, right here, so it must be attributed somewhere, not discarded). The alternative (PRD-literal `weight_ms=0`) was tested directly and produces 8.5%/6.3%/10.0% residual on the three golden fixtures — failing G5's 2% tolerance for a reason that would recur on every well-instrumented run this product ever analyses, not a defect the run under test has. **This is an interpretive call on a genuine PRD contradiction, not a silent resolution** — surfaced by the independent review, decided here rather than reverted, per the owner's explicit "make the repair necessary... move onto the next step" instruction. **Flagged for a PRD amendment**: §16.1.1's `Run boundary` weight row should read "gap to `run_start`/`run_end`", or §16.2.3's 2% tolerance should be revisited for runs with unavoidable lead-in/trail-off, whichever the PRD's own author intends — this ruling does not resolve which, only that the code's current behavior is deliberate and documented | `timing.py`'s `_build_edges`, `run_boundary` block; `overhead.py`'s `_classify_edge` (routes `run_boundary` weight to `blocking_wait`); `docs/performance-analysis.md` §2, §4.4 |
| C-20 | PRD §16.4 names `agent.busy_ms`/`agent.idle_ms` as "Occupancy" with no formula for either — genuinely silent, not merely terse (no other PRD section elaborates). Found while building `aggregates.py` (the repair that closes the review's finding #2, §16.4 being unimplemented) | **An agent's occupancy window is `[min(start of its own nodes), max(end of its own nodes)]` — the span it was observably in play. `busy_ms` = Σ its own node durations over the whole DAG (not just the critical path); `idle_ms` = whatever of that window `busy_ms` does not cover.** Time before an agent's first node or after its last is counted as neither busy nor idle — the agent is not yet, or no longer, part of the run. An agent active in one contiguous span has `idle_ms == 0` by construction; a real between-turns gap inside the window is what registers | `aggregates.py`'s module docstring §"agent.busy_ms/idle_ms"; `tests/analysis/test_aggregates.py`'s hand-computed `alpha`/`beta` case (`idle_ms = 5` / `0`) |
| C-21 | PRD §17.3's six-bucket gap attribution formula (`attribution_b = -gap * marginal_b / Σmarginal`) is undefined when Σmarginal = 0 — every bucket has zero critical-path duration, but `achieved_speedup` (measured against an independently-generated baseline) and `ideal_parallel_speedup` (intrinsic to the run's own DAG decomposition) are not forced to agree, so `gap` can still be nonzero. PRD §17.3 is silent on this case; Design Constraint 2 requires the sum to close and to "report honestly when it does not." Found at P11 build time via a genuine fan-out test fixture (`_fanout_log`/`_fanout_baseline`, zero overhead in all six buckets, `achieved_speedup=2.2` vs. `ideal_parallel_speedup=1.4`) | **The entire `overhead_cost` is assigned to the `unattributed` bucket; all five named buckets get 0.** Σattribution still equals `overhead_cost` exactly (the invariant `compare()` asserts and raises `E-BASE-002` on if violated), and the number is not silently discarded or fabricated across buckets that measurably contributed nothing. The alternative (raising `E-BASE-002` whenever this occurs) was rejected: it is not a data-quality defect, it is the honest, expected shape of a genuinely overhead-free run compared against a baseline whose own speedup has no forced relationship to this run's decomposition | `baseline.py`'s `_attribute_gap`, `total_marginal == 0.0` branch and its module docstring; `docs/baseline-methodology.md` §4.2; `tests/analysis/test_baseline.py::test_compare_signed_six_bucket_attribution_sums_to_the_overhead_cost` |

C-11's substantive ruling — an absent `version`/`target` key is not automatically an error, and §21.2's worked example is normative for that question — still stands and is unaffected. What was false was narrower: the specific claim that PRD's literal text, unedited, validates with zero errors. A second independent OP-2, auditing this same repair the same day, reproduced the identical bug live against `fixtures/perturbations/` (a second real, shipped, non-fixture directory) and against a wholly fictional fixture name — both silently inferred a bogus `target.fixture` exactly as the original finding described, because neither was in the one-item denylist. This is the same shape of defect as the C-11 citation error above, recurring in the very ruling that corrected it. Fixed under **C-15** (§10): `infer_fixture_from_task_path`/`non_fixture_task_dir` now check `fixtures/<name>/graph.py` exists on disk, a positive check that generalises correctly rather than a name list requiring maintenance. `docs/scenario-reference.md`'s matching overclaim is also corrected. See **C-15** for the corrected mechanism and its regression coverage.

## 11. Drift tripwires — stop and run OP-3 if you observe any of these

1. A test is changed to make it pass, rather than the code being changed. **Especially** a determinism or false-positive test.
2. `sleep`, `time.time()`, `random`, `uuid4()` or `datetime.now()` without the seeded/virtual source appears under `src/agentdx/` outside the sanctioned points in `AGENTS.md` §4.
3. An `analysis/` module imports from `runtime/`, `sdk/`, or any model client.
4. A verdict, finding or scorecard is produced without event `seq` references in its evidence array.
5. A threshold, weight or magic number appears inline in code instead of `verdict_rules.toml` / `agentdx.toml`.
6. The event schema is modified after week 1 without an ADR and a `schema_version` bump.
7. A number appears in the README, docs or UI with no `[bench:<file>]` marker resolving to `bench/results/`.
8. A panel starts owning selection state instead of the Zustand store.
9. Someone proposes an LLM-as-judge, semantic dedup, hosted/auth features, production monitoring, a fifth+ MVP fault type, or a v1 report exporter (§10 C-2) — all are explicitly out of scope.
9b. The `tests/false_positives/` k=2 harness grows a CLI flag, an output format or a reduction report. It is a test fixture, not an early FR-6 (ADR-002).
10. A scope cut is taken out of order (§12) or below the hard floor.
11. A prompt's output covers modules its `DELIVERABLES` list did not name.
12. A `replay`-mode cache miss is handled by anything other than a hard error.
13. A row is edited or removed from §8 or §9.
15. **A workflow on `main` is red, or has been red across more than one run.** The determinism job was red on the P02 commit, on a scheduled nightly, and on P03 before anyone read the badge — three runs of a job whose two gates, G2 and G3, are on PRD §44.3's never-waived list (D-19). A gate that is permanently red is a gate everyone learns to scroll past, which is worse than not having it. Check the Actions tab as part of the §1 opening ritual, not only after a push.
14. **A PRD requirement inside a completed prompt's scope was neither implemented nor declared in §9.** Every tripwire above catches doing *too much* — 11 catches over-scope, 9 catches out-of-scope features. **None catches doing too little**, and that asymmetry is the hole PRD §27.3's "or 50 ms" fell through: it was never built, never declared, and no gate noticed for a whole prompt (D-16, found by an OP-2 rather than by CI). The cheap mechanisation is a test *named for the requirement* — under-implementation is invisible precisely because nothing names it. Note that a citation checker would **not** have caught this: all 53 `PRD §n` references in `src/` resolve to sections that exist, and the defect was a *topical* mis-citation (`events/writer.py` justified its batching by citing §39.6, the release process, which never mentions batching).
16. **A recording or proxy view subclasses a CPython builtin (`dict`, `list`, `set`) directly rather than the corresponding `collections.abc` type**, letting C-level fast paths — `dict.update`, `{**x}`, `dict(x)`, `list(x)`, `x in s` — bypass the Python-level `__getitem__` / `__contains__` / `keys` overrides the capture depends on. This is how P04's `RecordingStateView` recorded **zero** `state_read` events for `dict(state)`, with no gap event and a green suite (D-26): the object passed every `isinstance` check and answered every question correctly, and simply was not asked. Greppable: `rg 'class \w+\((dict|list|set)\b' src/agentdx/`. Note that the safe direction costs something real — the ABC is not a `dict`, so any call site doing `isinstance(x, dict)` must be checked in the same change.
17. **A code change moves which side of an acceptance-gate threshold a fixture lands on, and the commit's own account frames it only as a "bug fix," with no corresponding §9 deviation row or §10 ruling added in the same commit.** This is how P10's `run_boundary` weight change shipped: it took a gate from failing (8.5%/6.3%/10.0% residual, literal PRD weight) to passing (0% residual, the code's actual weight) on every golden fixture, narrated only as correcting a bug, with no D-n/C-n entry until a same-session independent review found it (**D-50**, **C-19**). Any diff that flips a gate's pass/fail status is a deviation-or-ruling event by definition, whatever the author calls it — rerun the gate's test before/after the diff; if the verdict changed and neither §9 nor §10 gained a row in the same commit, this tripwire fires.
18. **A function enforcing a numbered invariant (I1–I13) has a call site where an exception handler, a `None`/missing-row check, or an early return causes the function to be *skipped* rather than causing the operation to be refused.** Found by an OP-2-style review of P14's `api/routes/runs.py::inject_fault` (2026-08-24): `_check_chaos_authorization` (I12) implemented the right rule internally, but its call site defaulted to `resolved = {}` whenever the run's scenario record was missing or failed to re-parse — `_is_graph_target({})` then read as "not a graph target," so the authorization check was skipped entirely rather than the fault being refused, arming faults against unverifiable runs with zero I12 enforcement. Repaired same day: `ScenarioUnresolvableForChaosError` (`409 E-CHAOS-004`) now refuses whenever `scenario_id` is set but unresolvable — see the repair's own regression tests (`tests/api/test_runs.py`, the I12 authorization block) for the full matrix this gap had zero coverage of before. **A related, narrower gap was found and deliberately left open, not silently closed**: a run whose `scenario_id` was never populated at all still skips the check — unreachable through the shipped `POST /api/runs` today (which always sets it), but a real gap if some other path ever creates a run without one; closing it is a product decision (what should an unassociated run's fault-authorization default be?), not a repair, so it stays open. For every I-numbered invariant's own enforcement function, check each call site: does every non-happy-path branch upstream of the call end in a refusal, or can one route around the check by way of a missing row, a caught exception, or an early return?
19. **An acceptance-gate test that shells out to a subprocess trusts the exit code alone as "the property held," with nothing checking that the subprocess actually exercised any real assertion.** Found by independent review of `tests/acceptance/test_gates.py` (2026-08-27 audit, repaired 2026-08-29, D-70): G2 and G3 — PRD §44.3's two never-waived gates — ran `pytest <suite>` and asserted only `returncode == 0`. Since pytest returns 0 when every individual test is skipped at runtime (a broken fixture, an environment guard — anything short of *zero tests collected*, which is the only all-skip case pytest itself treats as non-zero, exit 5), a suite that silently skipped every test would report the gate PASS with nothing real behind it. Repaired via `_run_gate`'s new `min_pytest_passed` parameter, which parses the captured stdout's "N passed" summary line and fails the gate if N is below the given floor — proven against both a fabricated all-skipped case and a fabricated real-pass case before being trusted (see the D-70 verification). This is tripwire 7's blind spot in a different shape: 7 catches a *missing* `[bench:<file>]` marker; this catches a command that *exits 0 for the wrong reason*. Sibling risk, not yet mechanised: the same question applies to any other acceptance or CI gate that shells out and trusts only an exit code — audit each one the same way before trusting it.

---

## 12. Scope-cut order — if the schedule slips, cut in exactly this order

1. FR-6 bounded exploration — the shipped feature only. **The `tests/false_positives/` k=2 harness is P0 and is never cut with it (ADR-002); G2 remains fully met after this cut**
2. FR-11b CI mode
3. FR-9 resilience scoring *(row 11b, not the whole of P11)*
4. Graph panel — the waterfall carries the demo alone *(this also forces a G8 amendment, §5)*
5. OTel export
6. Support-triage fixture — **the healthy control (`research_fanout`) is never cut**

**Hard floor:** FR-1, FR-2, FR-3, FR-5, FR-7, FR-8, FR-12 (`code_pipeline` + `research_fanout`), waterfall + scorecard.
Below this floor the thesis is not demonstrable. **Ship late rather than incomplete.**

*Note: FR-4 (fault injection) is P0 but does not appear in the PRD's hard floor. That is the PRD's text (§40.3), preserved. It means the floor is "the speedup thesis", not "the chaos thesis" — do not read its absence as permission to cut FR-4 casually; cutting it needs an ADR.*

## 14. Handoff brief — regenerate before moving to a different AI or a new session

> **Paste this section, plus §1–§5 and §8–§12, into the new assistant.**

*Regenerated 2026-08-16, after P08's build and two consecutive independent OP-2/OP-3 cycles (`scenario/`: FAIL/repair, then FAIL/repair again — the second audit caught a defect inside the first repair itself; a third re-audit is still owed). Stale the moment §5 changes — rewrite it, don't trust it.*

- **What this is:** a pre-deployment coordination debugger for multi-agent AI systems — deterministic scheduler, LLM record/replay, fault injection, append-only event log, pure analysis over that log. Week 3 of 10, solo build.

- **What is built and verified:** `fixtures/` (P05) only. `VERIFIED` requires an independent OP-2 that *passes* (§0). Six independent OP-2s have now run — `sdk/` at P04 (**FAIL**, repaired, re-audit owed), `fixtures/` at P05 (**PASS WITH NOTES**, §5 row 5 promoted to `VERIFIED`), `runtime/` at P06 (**FAIL**, repaired same day, re-audit owed), `runtime/cache/` at P07 (**FAIL**, repaired same day, re-audit owed), and `scenario/` at P08 **twice** (first: **FAIL**, 6 findings, repaired same day; second, re-auditing that repair: **FAIL** again, 5 findings including a defect inside the first repair itself, repaired same day — a third re-audit is owed, see §13's newest row).

- **What is built but unverified:**
  - **P01** repo scaffold, toolchain, seven CI gates. Green on GitHub and, since P03, green on macOS/arm64/CPython 3.12.2 including `typecheck`.
  - **P02** `events/` — the event contract, and the module everything else depends on. Its `docs/event-schema.md` (610 lines) is the human-readable contract; **read it before touching anything in `events/`, and before writing anything that consumes an event log.**
  - **P03** `store/` — persistence, plus an OP-2 (self-run, not independent — §0) that returned FAIL and an OP-3 that repaired it. `docs/storage.md` is its contract. **A real, independent audit is still owed** and should assume the self-repair did not find everything. Four things a consumer must know: ① `Store` *is* the `EventSink`; ② `create_run(RunRecord)` must happen **before** the first `append` (`E-STORE-004`); ③ the store never validates, canonicalises, or reads a clock; ④ append-only is enforced by SQLite triggers on every connection.
  - **P04** `sdk/` — the capture surface. `docs/sdk.md` is its contract. An independent OP-2 returned FAIL on eleven findings, all repaired; **a re-audit is owed.** ① The SDK builds `DraftEvent`s and **cannot stamp** (`test_sdk_never_stamps.py` enforces this against the AST — P06 is the module that now does stamp); ② it never reads a clock; ③ `capture_bodies` defaults to the resolved `[privacy]` setting (I8); ④ a failed binding is fatal and always emits an `instrumentation_gap` + warning + `RunResult.gaps` entry.
  - **P06** `runtime/` — the scheduler, virtual clock, determinism traps, and run context. **Audited the same day it was built; independent OP-2 returned FAIL; repaired same session. Re-audit still owed.** `docs/determinism-guarantees.md` is its contract. Gate G3 passes locally. The build session's own bug-finding (deadlocked `yield_point`, the internal-dispatch/virtual-`asyncio.sleep` collision) was real but incomplete — the independent auditor found two more, structural ones, that self-review had missed: the vector clock was not per-slot (PRD §14.2) and `_SchedulerRecorder` did not satisfy `sdk.generic.Recorder` at all, meaning no SDK-authored event could ever have reached a real scheduler. Both are fixed now. Five things a consumer must know: ① `Scheduler.stamp(draft, causes=())` / `scheduler.recorder.emit(draft, causes)` are the **only** places a `Stamp` is constructed — the SDK's `DraftEvent`s flow through here, not around it, and `causes` (not just the previous event) drives both `causal_parents` and the vclock merge; ② `yield_point()` is preemption (re-enters `RUNNABLE` immediately), `sleep()` is the only genuine block; ③ the virtual clock only advances when nothing is runnable; ④ `DeterminismGuard` is installed for the whole of `run()` and un-installs even on an exception, but its blind spots (a name captured before the guard installs, an unpatchable C extension) are real and documented in `docs/determinism-guarantees.md` §9; ⑤ `sdk.generic.RunHost` — "the half of `agentdx.run` that P06 owns" — is still unbuilt. This is a known, flagged gap (root cause (d)), not an oversight; it needs an owner decision, not a guess.
  - **P07** `runtime/cache/` — record/replay/perturb (key, store, modes, perturb strategies). **Audited the same day it was built; independent OP-2 returned FAIL; repaired same session (§13). Re-audit still owed.** `docs/cache.md` is its contract. 94/94 cache tests pass locally. Five things a consumer must know: ① `key.py`'s hash is proven equivalent to the SDK's own key algorithm only for `bool`/`int`/`str`/`bytes`/non-float-`set` values — a `float`-valued significant param diverges by design (canonical JSON refuses raw floats; the SDK embeds one bare), declared not hidden; ② a value with no reproducible `repr()` now raises `KeyMaterialError` (`E-CACHE-011`) instead of silently keying on a memory address — the defect the OP-2 found; ③ `redact` is a real, tested capability in `key.py` but the live SDK call site does not call this module at all, so `redact_patterns` has zero live effect until that (out-of-scope) file is touched; ④ `E-CACHE-002` means "Cache DB corrupt" and is now backed by a real `response_hash` check on every read, not a repurposed WAL-mode code (`E-CACHE-012` now); ⑤ `sdk.generic.RunHost`/the scheduler's `CacheHook` interception point (P06's still-open gap) mean neither this cache nor P06's hook fire in a real run yet — both are exercised directly by tests, not end to end.
  - **P08** `scenario/` — YAML schema, validation, matrix expansion, the pluggable assertion hook. **Audited twice, independently, the same day it was built — both FAIL, both repaired same session. A third re-audit is still owed.** `docs/scenario-reference.md` is its contract. 108/108 tests pass locally (up from 75, across two repair passes). **First OP-2** found no fault-parameter value validation at all (PRD §12.2's Safety-row bounds existed only in prose), a silent `EDGE`-target gap in `E-SCEN-005`, a `fixtures/tasks/` target-mis-inference bug, `tool_failure` declaring a trigger kind PRD §12.3 doesn't list for it, dead `[scenario]` guard-default config, and unvalidated matrix-expansion output — all six repaired, two (**C-13**, **C-14**) as new PRD rulings. **Second OP-2, re-auditing that repair the same day, found the first repair's own C-13 fix was too narrow** (a denylist of one name, `"tasks"`, still silently mis-inferring `fixtures/perturbations/` exactly the same way — reproduced live against a second real, shipped directory), that `target.fixture` was never checked for existence at all (typo/fictional names passed silently), that the new Safety-bound tests only checked extreme sentinels rather than the real PRD ceiling (proven by a monkeypatch that dropped a bound from 16 to 6 without failing the existing test), and that `run_python_success_check` had no timeout though PRD §21.6 requires one for both `success_check` forms. All five repaired: `infer_fixture_from_task_path` rewritten as a positive `fixtures/<name>/graph.py`-exists check (not a denylist); new `E-SCEN-012`/**C-15** rejects any nonexistent `target.fixture`; exact-boundary tests added; both success-check timeouts unified and read from `agentdx.toml`. Six things a consumer must know: ① nothing here executes a fault or a success check against a real run — `runtime/faults/` (P09) and a real `RunSummary` producer (P10/P11) are what make a validated scenario actually run; ② the I12 chaos-safety gate (`E-SCEN-004`) only applies to `target.graph` (a user-owned graph) — a `target.fixture` scenario needs no `chaos_opt_in`/`blast_radius`, since fixtures are already a sandboxed set; ③ `load_success_check`/`run_python_success_check` import and execute a `checks.py` ref — sanctioned for a fixture's own checks or a scenario's own committed file, **never** for a ref resolved from inside an imported `.agentdx` bundle (Design Constraint 5's trust boundary, documented in `assertions.py`'s module docstring — `store/bundle.py`/`cli/` are the modules responsible for keeping bundle-derived refs away from this loader); ④ guard ceilings (`E-SCEN-008`) are read from `agentdx.toml`'s new `[scenario]` section, not hardcoded — a value above the ceiling is an error even though it is below the PRD's *default*, which only sets the starting point, not the limit; ⑤ fault parameter values and trigger fields are validated against PRD §12.2/§12.3's declared bounds at their exact boundaries (`ParamConstraint`, `TRIGGER_FIELD_CONSTRAINTS`) — **except** `agent_crash`'s "cannot crash the last live agent unless `allow_total_failure: true`" rule, a cross-fault runtime invariant this module cannot check statically, deliberately left as a P09 precondition; ⑥ `target.fixture` (inferred or explicit) must now name a real fixture on disk (`E-SCEN-012`) — `target.graph` is never checked this way, since a user-owned graph is legitimately resolved best-effort.

- **What is next:** **P09 — `runtime/faults/`** (4 MVP faults) + chaos safety. Gate **G4**. `scenarios/kill_reviewer.yaml` already declares the exact fault G4 needs; P09 is what makes it actually fire. ADR-001 consequence 2's fixture-golden regeneration is still owed — the cache now exists, but the regeneration itself (replacing `fixtures/_harness.py` with a real end-to-end run) has not been done; not blocking, but should not be forgotten.

- **What is fragile right now:**
  - **`scenario/` has had two audits, and each found real defects the previous repair missed — including, the second time, a defect inside the first repair itself.** Every prior module's first independent OP-2 has found something real — three high findings in `bundle.py` (P03), eleven in `sdk/` (P04), a vector-clock defect and a broken `Recorder` protocol in `runtime/` (P06), a process-local key-material leak in `runtime/cache/` (P07), six in `scenario/`'s first OP-2 (P08) — but `scenario/` is now also the first module where a *second* independent audit, run specifically to re-check the first repair, still came back FAIL: the C-13 fix (denylist one name) was narrower than its own ruling claimed, and a second real non-fixture directory (`fixtures/perturbations/`) reproduced the identical bug. **Do not assume a repair pass found everything just because it addressed every named finding — this module is direct proof that a repair can pass its own new tests while still under-fixing the underlying defect.** Two areas neither audit has touched, still worth an adversary's attention: the I12 chaos-safety gate (`E-SCEN-004` — does it actually block every route to an unbounded or unauthorised fault, or only the ones this session's own tests thought to try?) and the AST-based graph-identity resolution (`resolve_graph_identity` — does "never import the target" actually hold under a crafted `graph.py`, or does some path still execute user code?). Also still open: `agent_crash`'s "cannot crash the last live agent" Safety-row rule is declared, not enforced — it is a P09 (fault-execution) precondition, not a `scenario/` validation gap, and P09 must not assume `scenario/`'s validation already covers it. A third re-audit is owed before `VERIFIED`.
  - **`runtime/cache/` has had one audit, and it found a real I1-adjacent defect.** The independent OP-2 (§13) returned FAIL on a process-local key-material bug, an overloaded error code, an unverified integrity field, and two tests that couldn't have caught what they claimed to — all repaired same session, but a re-audit has not yet happened. Treat the repair with the same suspicion the audit itself deserved, same as every other module. The float/set key-equivalence gap and the un-wired redaction/`RunHost`/`CacheHook` points are **declared, not fixed** — see the P07 bullet above.
  - **This P06 session ran with no `.git` in the build environment at all** (a cloud-sandbox transfer of the repo, made because the owner's local device_bash had a broken venv symlink and no network access). Everything reported green here — `ruff`, `mypy`, `pytest`, `import-linter`, `check_determinism_hygiene`, `check_bench_markers`, `check_fixture_finding_evidence`, the three `fixtures-check` hashes — was run and is real, but `check_ledger.py` **could not run at all** (`fatal: not a git repository`), and none of it has been confirmed in an actual CI job. **Re-run `just ci` in full on the real repository before trusting this session's green state as CI-confirmed.**
  - **`runtime/` has had one audit, and it found real structural defects.** The independent OP-2 (§13) returned FAIL on a vector-clock defect and a `Recorder` protocol mismatch, both repaired same session — but a re-audit has not yet happened, so treat the repair with the same suspicion the audit itself deserved: every module audited so far has had something found (P03: 3 high in `bundle.py`; P04: 11, six of them silent-capture holes; P05: 5 more after a self-audit already found 5; P06: 2 structural + 2 minor), and there is no reason to assume this repair pass found everything either. The `RunHost` gap it also found is **still open** — not a defect to silently close, a decision the owner has not made yet.
  - **D-08:** `events/` and `store/` are written in a 3.10-parseable dialect (`TypeAlias`, `ValueEnum`) because the original build host had no 3.12. Behaviour is identical. **Do not "modernise" it to PEP 695 / `StrEnum` without checking the host first.** Still owed.
  - **`_sealed_at` in `store/sqlite.py`** derives its timestamp from the log rather than a clock — now that `runtime/clock.py`'s `wall_time()` exists and is exported (P06), this is cheaper to fix than it was at P03.
  - **Not exercised yet, because no run host exists to wire them together:** no fixture has been run through the real scheduler or the real cache (P05's fixtures still stamp via `fixtures/_harness.py`), a bundle's cache slice is still derived from `llm_call` payloads rather than from a real `cache.db`, and `runtime.scheduler.Scheduler` never actually calls the `CacheHook` interception point P07 implements. `agentdx import --verify`'s re-execution is deliberately absent from `store/` — P17 owes it.

- **What must not be touched:** the §2 invariants and the §3 locked decisions. Three specifics that are easy to break by accident: **the canonical projection is derived from the volatility marks in `events/schema.py` — never write a second exclusion list** (§4, C-7); **set-valued arrays must be emitted sorted** by their emitter (`E-EVENT-028`), because a canonicaliser that reordered would hide a nondeterministic emitter; and **no threshold may appear inline in code** — `store/` has a test that greps itself to prove it (tripwire 5).

- **Known open decisions the new assistant must not guess at:** §10's remaining `Q-43.2.*` rows (calibration defaults, DPOR, canvas switchover, verdict weights, `state_read` sampling). Q-43.2.8 is now closed by ADR-008 and Q-43.2.2's default is implemented and configurable. **Q-P02.1 is Accepted but only by owner delegation, not independent review** — the ten payload schemas PRD §9.5 never specified were derived by the same agent that accepted them. Treat them as the softest part of the contract and the first target of a real OP-2.

- **Process rule most often broken here:** if the PRD contradicts itself, or is silent on something load-bearing, **stop and ask** (`AGENTS.md` §3). P02 hit four such cases before writing a line of code and two would have made gate G3 permanently unpassable if guessed; P03 hit three and one would have made stored logs unable to verify their own hash chain. The pattern is consistent: the expensive conflicts surface *before* implementation, not during it.
